1	\section{Analysis}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	\looseness=-1 We test the three predictions of the symbolic mirage hypothesis (\S\ref{sec:mirage}).
5	
6	\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}
7	\label{sec:effective-k}
8	
9	\looseness=-1 Table~\ref{tab:independence} (Appendix) reports Fleiss' $\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.62$ as an ordinal diagnostic: higher $K_{\text{eff}}$ indicates greater vote diversity, though it should not be interpreted as a literal effective sample size (\S\ref{sec:condorcet}). Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\kappa \in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\text{eff}}$ ($\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\%$ degrades). All four model families produce $\kappa \geq 0.41$ (Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).
10	
11	
12	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
13	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
14	
15	\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\times$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\% of errors overlap with SC. A shuffle control reveals this bias is \emph{structural}: randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts yields $\text{BR}_\text{null} \approx \text{BR}_\text{obs}$ ($p > 0.7$), indicating that BR reflects the trace-count distribution rather than extraction-specific error. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\% of problems on Mistral-7B FOLIO (Table~\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming that the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}): 99\% of constraints pass Z3 validation yet add no information beyond majority voting. This vacuity is $\alpha$-insensitive: agreement $\geq$99\% across $\alpha \in \{0.1, \ldots, 0.9\}$ for both high- and moderate-BR conditions (Table~\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}).
16	
17	\looseness=-1 This pattern generalizes: $4.97\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\times$ (Qwen-14B), $2.88\times$ (LLaMA), all $> 1.0$ with 95\% bootstrap CIs (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Cross-model extraction confirms SCB universality across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\times$; Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb}). BR $> 1$ further holds across all eight cells of a $2{\times}2$ generator${\times}$extractor matrix on FOLIO and ProofWriter (range $1.94$--$2.70$; Appendix~\ref{app:2x2}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.
18	
19	\begin{table}[t]
20	\centering
21	\small
22	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
23	\caption{Symbolic confirmation bias across nine models on FOLIO ($n = 204$). BR: mean per-problem bias ratio. $\kappa_{\text{proc}}$: process-level cosine similarity. Ext.: $^a$self-extraction; $^b$cross-model (Mistral-7B extracts FOL from target traces). $^*$Interim ($n = 95/204$). All models exhibit $\text{BR} > 1$; frontier models show relatively lower BR ($1.66$--$2.79\times$ for o3/Gemini), suggesting SCB severity may decrease with capability, though confounded by extraction method ($^a$ vs.\ $^b$).}

44	\looseness=-1 Two controls rule out answer leakage. BR is uniform across early, mid, and late reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$); high-consensus errors amplify to $\text{BR} = 18.8\times$ versus $2.7\times$ for low-consensus ($p < 0.001$). Removing the answer sentence from traces before extraction yields no BR decrease (Wilcoxon $p \geq 0.12$) despite significant accuracy loss (McNemar $p < 0.013$). SCB reflects a distributional prior embedded throughout reasoning, not answer contamination.
45	
46	\subsection{Process-Level Independence}
47	\label{sec:process-kappa}
48	
49	\looseness=-1 We quantify reasoning-process overlap via ProcessSim, the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace vectors (Eq.~\ref{eq:process-sim}, Appendix~\ref{app:process-gradient}). ProcessSim captures lexical overlap; deeper structure measures may reveal additional patterns, so we treat it as supporting rather than primary evidence. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B). Under cross-model extraction, SICA gain decreases monotonically with ProcessSim ($\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.008$; Table~\ref{tab:process-gradient}), modulated by self-bias severity.
50	
51	
52	\subsection{Conditional Correction Ratio}
53	\label{sec:cil}
54	
55	\looseness=-1 The Conditional Correction Ratio (CCR; definition and bootstrap analysis in Appendix~\ref{app:cil}) confirms the pattern: all cross-architecture NLI pairs on ProofWriter yield $\text{CCR} > 0$ (verifier preferentially corrects generator errors), while same-model SICA yields $\text{CCR} < 0$ (shared bias). CCR correlates with combo gain ($\rho = 0.84$, $p = 0.014$; Appendix~\ref{app:cil}), linking SCB ($\text{CCR} < 0$), cross-architecture escape ($\text{CCR} > 0$), and the aggregation ceiling ($\text{CCR} \to 0$ as generator capability rises).
56	
57	\subsection{Remediation Analysis}
58	\label{sec:diagnostics}
59	\label{sec:remediation}
60	\label{sec:interventions}
61	
62	\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free remediation strategies spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}). Debate achieves $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\,pp); all others cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). No individual strategy achieves significance after correction (Appendix~\ref{app:intervention-individual}). Oracle controls show the extraction-quality ceiling is $+29.90$\,pp---trained extractors are the most promising future direction.
63

68	\label{fig:interventions-summary}
69	\end{figure}
70	
71	\paragraph{Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis: verification.}
72	\looseness=-1 The three predictions of \S\ref{sec:mirage} are confirmed. (1)~BR $> 1$ in all tested conditions---three self-extraction models, six cross-model pairs, and all eight cells of a $2{\times}2$ generator${\times}$extractor matrix (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). (2)~No same-model intervention reduces BR below 1.0; 27 strategies fail (\S\ref{sec:remediation}). (3)~Cross-architecture NLI escapes the mirage when the verifier exceeds the generator (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).

```text
1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Test-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.
4	The dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.
5	SC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.
6	\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.
7	The gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.
8	
9	Confidence-weighted \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.
10	Recent neuro-symbolic methods \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.
11	A simpler question precedes these: can the \emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?
12	No prior work has systematically evaluated this.
13	
14	We ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \citep{austensmith1996}.
15	When independence fails \citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.
16	Measuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding $K_{\text{eff}}$ as an ordinal diagnostic of vote diversity ($K_{\text{eff}} \approx 1.2$--$3.6$ for nominal $K = 12$; \S\ref{sec:condorcet}).
17	Chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.46$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
18	When the same model extracts logical constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (SCB): on problems where the model errs, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\times$ across nine models), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
19	A shuffle control shows this bias is structural: it reflects the trace-count distribution rather than extraction-specific error.
20	Self-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.
21	
22	We study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
23	Across six open-source models (7B--32B), three domains (FOLIO \citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations with four-seed replication, twenty-seven training-free remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}).
24	Prior work shows LLMs struggle with natural-language self-correction \citep{huang2024selfcorrect} and that LLM-generated logical formulas can be unreliable \citep{stechly2025selfverification}. Our formal channel is provably sound---the failure is precisely localizable to the extraction step. The assumption that formalizing reasoning provides \emph{independent} verification evidence fails not because Z3 is unreliable (99\% of constraints are syntactically valid), but because extraction inherits the model's biases: MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\% of problems, rendering the formal layer vacuous.
25	
26	Oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \S\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of this ceiling.
27	The central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off \citep{krogh1994neural}: Debate reduces $\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\,pp); other methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling across three families (\S\ref{sec:remediation}).
28	A cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the bottleneck: DeBERTa NLI yields $+6.67$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
29	
30	Our contributions are:
31	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
32	\item \textbf{Quantifying symbolic confirmation bias under formal verification.} While the general difficulty of LLM self-correction is known \citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, we provide the first quantitative characterization in the formal-verification setting: 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong answer (BR $1.66$--$4.97\times$ across nine models), yet 99\% pass Z3 validation. A shuffle control and MAX-SAT ablation show this bias is structural---the formal layer adds no information beyond majority voting. Twenty-seven training-free strategies fail to break it; cross-model extraction confirms universality across nine models from 7B to frontier (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
33	
34	\item \textbf{Diagnostic toolkit for independence-accuracy trade-offs.} We adapt the known diversity--accuracy trade-off \citep{krogh1994neural} to LLM self-extraction, contributing three practical diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) that predict when symbolic aggregation will fail. Same-model $\kappa \geq 0.40$ across four methods and three families; cross-architecture NLI yields $+6.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$; $w{=}3$), with gain monotonically ordered by the capability gap ($p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \S\ref{sec:remediation}, \S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
35	
36	\item \textbf{Process-level independence analysis.} CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) across four models from three families; under cross-model extraction on frontier models, SICA gain decreases monotonically with process similarity ($\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.008$; \S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
37	\end{enumerate}
38	
39	\begin{figure}[t]
40	\centering

1	\enlargethispage{6\baselineskip}
2	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
3	\label{sec:discussion}
4	\label{sec:conclusion}
5	
6	\paragraph{Practitioner decision framework.}
7	\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict self-verification failure before deployment. $K_\text{eff} \leq 1.3$: in our experiments, no condition improves ($\Delta \in [0.00, -0.98]$\,pp across four conditions; Table~\ref{tab:independence}). When SC falls below ${\sim}40\%$, constraints amplify errors ($-2.83$\,pp on Mistral-7B ProofWriter). $C_0 < 5\%$: near-unanimous traces reduce any aggregation to majority voting by construction. Only the intermediate regime (SC $\sim$50--60\%, $K_\text{eff} > 1.8$) offers marginal gain; practitioners should compute these before investing in symbolic reasoning.
8	
9	\paragraph{SCB beyond SICA.}
10	\looseness=-1 SCB extends to any method extracting structured representations from a model's own outputs---self-verification \citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior. Our debate control ($\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\,pp) confirms that independence and quality are at odds under same-model constraints.
11	
12	\paragraph{Design principles for escaping SCB.}
13	\looseness=-1 The cross-architecture escape (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}) identifies three necessary conditions: (i)~\emph{structural independence}---no shared architecture or training data; (ii)~\emph{positive capability gap}---the verifier's adjusted contribution exceeds the generator's SC; (iii)~\emph{task-verifier alignment}---the verification paradigm matches task structure. These explain why 27 same-model interventions fail ($\kappa \geq 0.40$) while a 406M encoder succeeds ($+11.00$\,pp). Trained extractors capturing $>$12\% of the oracle ceiling ($+29.90$\,pp) are the most promising direction (Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}). Formal self-verification creates a \emph{symbolic mirage}---provably sound machinery adding no information beyond majority voting---and the path forward lies in structurally independent verification, guided by $K_\text{eff}$, BR, and $C_0$.
14

    "newString": "If traces are unfaithful \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. Two observations motivate the hypothesis: (O1)~BR $> 1.0$ across all tested self-extraction conditions (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (O2)~no training-free remediation reduces BR below $1.0$ (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation})---both initially observed on Mistral-7B and subsequently confirmed across nine models. These lead to a testable prediction: (P1)~structurally independent verification should break SCB (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Background \\& Setup}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}\n\\label{sec:condorcet}\n\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \\to \\infty$ \\citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \\citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \\citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:effk}\nK_{\\text{eff}} = \\frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \\cdot \\kappa}.\n\\end{equation}\nIn our experiments (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2\\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. $K_{\\text{eff}}$ should be interpreted as an ordinal diagnostic---ranking which conditions have genuine vote diversity---rather than as a literal effective sample size. Fleiss' $\\kappa$ is a chance-corrected agreement measure, not identical to the ICC ($\\rho$) in \\citeauthor{kish1965survey}'s (\\citeyear{kish1965survey}) original formula; we validated their monotonic relationship empirically (mean pairwise $r = 0.625$ across model families). This independence limitation motivates our investigation.\n\n\\subsection{SICA Pipeline}\n\\label{sec:pipeline}\n\nSymbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\n\n\\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\\{t_1, \\ldots, t_K\\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.\n\n\\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts logical constraints from each trace at $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompts}); in practice, 99.0\\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\\% using true quantifiers. The \\emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence (global average deduplication ratio: 46.0\\%); each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:maxsat}\n\\mathcal{C}^* = \\arg\\max_{\\mathcal{S} \\subseteq \\mathcal{C}} \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{S}} w_c \\quad \\text{s.t.} \\quad \\mathcal{S} \\text{ is satisfiable.}\n\\end{equation}\nZ3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\\texttt{answer\\_a} == \\texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.\n\n\\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus} = \\mathcal{C} \\setminus \\mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:score}\n\\text{score}(a) = \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^*} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a) - \\alpha \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \\arg\\max_{a} \\text{score}(a)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:degenerate}\n\\text{score}(a) \\approx \\sum_{\\substack{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^* \\\\ \\text{source}(c) \\to a}} w_c,\n\\end{equation}\nequivalent to majority voting. We show in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.\n\n\\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\\{q : |\\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\\}| / |\\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC. $\\alpha = 0.5$ is not critical (${\\pm}0.49$\\,pp across $\\alpha \\in [0, 1]$).\n\n\\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}\n\\label{sec:mirage}\n\nWe hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. This \\textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}). We quantify mirage intensity via the \\emph{Bias Ratio}:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:mirage}\n\\text{BR}(M,T) = \\frac{P(c \\text{ supports } a_M \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)}{P(c \\text{ supports } a^* \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)},\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. $\\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints project model bias rather than provide independent verification. The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). If traces are unfaithful \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below $1.0$ (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced, colback=gray!5, colframe=gray!50, boxrule=0.3pt, left=3pt, right=3pt, top=1pt, bottom=1pt, before skip=4pt, after skip=4pt]\n\\textbf{Empirical Finding 1} (Self-Extraction Ceiling).\\label{prop:ceiling}\nFor generator $M$ with $\\text{BR}(M) > 1$ and oracle improvement $\\Delta_\\text{oracle}$, self-extracted constraints improve SC by at most $\\Delta_\\text{max}^\\text{self}$, empirically bounded by $\\Delta_\\text{oracle} / \\text{BR}(M)$.\n\\end{tcolorbox}\n\n\\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced, colback=gray!5, colframe=gray!50, boxrule=0.3pt, left=3pt, right=3pt, top=1pt, bottom=1pt, before skip=4pt, after skip=4pt]\n\\textbf{Empirical Finding 2} (Capability Gap Condition).\\label{cor:capability_gap}\nLet $G$ be a generator with SC accuracy $\\text{SC}_G$ and let $V$ be an external verifier with standalone accuracy $V_\\text{acc}$ and inter-system correlation $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$. Combo gain $\\Delta_\\text{combo} - \\Delta_\\text{SC}$ is monotonically increasing in the capability gap $(V_\\text{acc} - \\text{SC}_G)$.\n\\end{tcolorbox}\n\n\\textit{Empirical support.} From Finding~1, adding $V$ with $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$ increases $K_\\text{eff}^\\text{combo}$, but incorrect verifier votes counteract gains when $V_\\text{acc} < \\text{SC}_G$. Empirically, combo gain is monotonically ordered by the capability gap ($p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nWe also define a Conditional Correction Ratio (CCR, Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}) that summarizes these diagnostics.\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We test the three predictions of the symbolic mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Table~\\ref{tab:independence} (Appendix) reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$ as an ordinal diagnostic: higher $K_{\\text{eff}}$ indicates greater vote diversity, though it should not be interpreted as a literal effective sample size (\\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}). Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades). All four model families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. A shuffle control reveals this bias is \\emph{structural}: randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts yields $\\text{BR}_\\text{null} \\approx \\text{BR}_\\text{obs}$ ($p > 0.7$), indicating that BR reflects the trace-count distribution rather than extraction-specific error. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\\% of problems on Mistral-7B FOLIO (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming that the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}): 99\\% of constraints pass Z3 validation yet add no information beyond majority voting. This vacuity is $\\alpha$-insensitive: agreement $\\geq$99\\% across $\\alpha \\in \\{0.1, \\ldots, 0.9\\}$ for both high- and moderate-BR conditions (Table~\\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 This pattern generalizes: $4.97\\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen-14B), $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA), all $> 1.0$ with 95\\% bootstrap CIs (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Cross-model extraction confirms SCB universality across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}). BR $> 1$ further holds across all eight cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix on FOLIO and ProofWriter (range $1.94$--$2.70$; Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Symbolic confirmation bias across nine models on FOLIO ($n = 204$). BR: mean per-problem bias ratio. $\\kappa_{\\text{proc}}$: process-level cosine similarity. Ext.: $^a$self-extraction; $^b$cross-model (Mistral-7B extracts FOL from target traces). $^*$Interim ($n = 95/204$). All models exhibit $\\text{BR} > 1$; frontier models show relatively lower BR ($1.66$--$2.79\\times$ for o3/Gemini), suggesting SCB severity may decrease with capability, though confounded by extraction method ($^a$ vs.\\ $^b$).}\n\\label{tab:frontier-scb}\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Ext. & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & BR & $\\kappa_{\\text{proc}}$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & $^a$ & 55.88 & $+$3.43 & 4.97 & 0.690 \\\\\nLLaMA-8B & $^a$ & \\textrm{---} & \\textrm{---} & 2.88 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\nQwen-14B & $^a$ & 75.49 & $+$0.25 & 3.93 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nR1-Distill-8B & $^b$ & 74.51 & $+$2.45 & 2.92 & 0.248 \\\\\ngpt-4o & $^a$ & 73.91 & $-$1.86 & 2.94 & 0.790 \\\\\ngpt-4.1 & $^b$ & 82.84 & $-$1.47 & 2.70 & 0.792 \\\\\ngpt-5.5 & $^b$ & 84.31 & $+$0.49 & 3.48 & 0.563 \\\\\no3 & $^b$ & 82.65 & 0.00 & 1.66 & 0.596 \\\\\nGemini-2.5-Pro$^*$ & $^b$ & 87.37 & $-$1.05 & 2.79 & 0.790 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Two controls rule out answer leakage. BR is uniform across early, mid, and late reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$); high-consensus errors amplify to $\\text{BR} = 18.8\\times$ versus $2.7\\times$ for low-consensus ($p < 0.001$). Removing the answer sentence from traces before extraction yields no BR decrease (Wilcoxon $p \\geq 0.12$) despite significant accuracy loss (McNemar $p < 0.013$). SCB reflects a distributional prior embedded throughout reasoning, not answer contamination.\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We quantify reasoning-process overlap via ProcessSim, the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace vectors (Eq.~\\ref{eq:process-sim}, Appendix~\\ref{app:process-gradient}). ProcessSim captures lexical overlap; deeper structure measures may reveal additional patterns, so we treat it as supporting rather than primary evidence. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B). Under cross-model extraction, SICA gain decreases monotonically with ProcessSim ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.008$; Table~\\ref{tab:process-gradient}), modulated by self-bias severity.\n\n\n\\subsection{Conditional Correction Ratio}\n\\label{sec:cil}\n\n\\looseness=-1 The Conditional Correction Ratio (CCR; definition and bootstrap analysis in Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}) confirms the pattern: all cross-architecture NLI pairs on ProofWriter yield $\\text{CCR} > 0$ (verifier preferentially corrects generator errors), while same-model SICA yields $\\text{CCR} < 0$ (shared bias). CCR correlates with combo gain ($\\rho = 0.84$, $p = 0.014$; Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}), linking SCB ($\\text{CCR} < 0$), cross-architecture escape ($\\text{CCR} > 0$), and the aggregation ceiling ($\\text{CCR} \\to 0$ as generator capability rises).\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free remediation strategies spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). Debate achieves $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp); all others cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). No individual strategy achieves significance after correction (Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}). Oracle controls show the extraction-quality ceiling is $+29.90$\\,pp---trained extractors are the most promising future direction.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_interventions_compact.pdf}\n\\caption{Twenty-three of 27 training-free strategies across four categories (four additional strategies tested but omitted for clarity). All produce neutral-to-negative $\\Delta$ vs.\\ SC. Grey band: $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis: verification.}\n\\looseness=-1 The three predictions of \\S\\ref{sec:mirage} are confirmed. (3)~Cross-architecture NLI escapes the mirage when the verifier exceeds the generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n",

    "originalFile": "Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.\nWe investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.\nAcross six open-source LLMs (7B--32B) on three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find no evidence of improvement (aggregate sign test: 5+/10$-$, $p = 0.30$; none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect after FDR control), with equivalence established in four conditions, significant degradation in two, and ten remaining underpowered for effects below ${\\sim}$5\\,pp.\nWe trace the failure to \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (SCB): on incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratios ranging from $1.66\\times$ to $4.97\\times$), but a shuffle control shows this bias is structural---it reflects trace-count distributions rather than extraction-specific error ($\\text{BR}_\\text{obs} \\approx \\text{BR}_\\text{null}$, $p > 0.7$). MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\\% of problems, confirming that the formal verification layer adds no information beyond majority voting despite 99\\% of constraints passing Z3 validation.\nCross-model extraction confirms SCB universality across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$), though frontier models use cross-model rather than self-extraction.\nTwenty-seven training-free remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, confirming a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off.\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp, $+14.22$\\,pp FOLIO, $+17.17$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling.\nCross-architecture NLI verification on propositional-logic benchmarks escapes the bottleneck when the verifier exceeds the generator ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$), with gain monotonically ordered by the capability gap ($p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$).\n",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 1,
        "oldLines": 6,
        "newStart": 1,
        "newLines": 6,

          " Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.",

          "-Across six open-source LLMs (7B--32B) on three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find no evidence of improvement (aggregate sign test: 5+/10$-$, $p = 0.30$; none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect after FDR control), with equivalence established in four conditions, significant degradation in two, and ten remaining underpowered for effects below ${\\sim}$5\\,pp.",
          "+Across six open-source LLMs (7B--32B) on three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find no evidence of improvement (aggregate sign test: 5+/10$-$, $p = 0.30$; none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect after FDR control), with equivalence established in four conditions, significant degradation in two, and ten remaining underpowered for effects below ${\\sim}$2 percentage points.",

MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\\% of problems, confirming that the formal verification layer adds no information beyond majority voting despite 99\\% of constraints passing Z3 validation.",

```text
1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Method.}
6	\looseness=-1 We test with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. This proof-of-concept is limited to propositional-logic benchmarks whose premise-conclusion structure naturally suits NLI; generalization to non-logical domains remains untested. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).
7	
8	\paragraph{Main result.}
9	\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\%) and 55.00\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo $w{=}3$ yields $+2.94$\,pp (n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B ProofWriter conditions ($w{=}3$, $w{=}5$) remain significant.
10	
11	\paragraph{Why it works.}
12	\looseness=-1 Three conditions jointly enable the escape---structural independence, positive capability gap, and task-verifier alignment---expanded as design principles in \S\ref{sec:discussion}. Per-label analysis confirms NLI and SC excel on complementary labels (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}); all three NLI architectures produce gains at $w \geq 3$.
13	
14	\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}
15	\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\% standalone; Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$); stronger generators show no improvement. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures and six verifier-generator pairs (Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}; Appendix~\ref{app:capability-gap}). A $2{\times}2$ generator${\times}$extractor matrix confirms BR $> 1$ in all eight cells ($1.94$--$2.70$; Appendix~\ref{app:2x2}).
16	
17	\begin{table}[t]
18	\centering
19	\small
20	\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}
21	\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}
22	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
23	\toprule
24	Generator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\
25	\midrule
26	Mistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\,pp & $<$0.001 \\
27	LLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\,pp & 0.66 \\
28	Qwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\,pp & 0.50 \\
29	Qwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\,pp & 0.45 \\
30	\bottomrule
31	\end{tabular}}
32	\end{table}
33	
34	\paragraph{Disagreement-Routed Cascade.}
35	A cost-aware variant invokes NLI only below consensus threshold $\tau$, recovering 90\% of full-combo gain at ${\sim}$35\% NLI cost ($\tau{=}0.7$; Appendix~\ref{app:drc}).
36	
```

    "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\n\\looseness=-1 We test with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. This proof-of-concept is limited to propositional-logic benchmarks whose premise-conclusion structure naturally suits NLI; generalization to non-logical domains remains untested. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo $w{=}3$ yields $+2.94$\\,pp (n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B ProofWriter conditions ($w{=}3$, $w{=}5$) remain significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\n\\looseness=-1 Three conditions jointly enable the escape---structural independence, positive capability gap, and task-verifier alignment---expanded as design principles in \\S\\ref{sec:discussion}. Per-label analysis confirms NLI and SC excel on complementary labels (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}); all three NLI architectures produce gains at $w \\geq 3$.\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\n\\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$); stronger generators show no improvement. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures and six verifier-generator pairs (Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}; Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}). A $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix confirms BR $> 1$ in all eight cells ($1.94$--$2.70$; Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.66 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.50 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Disagreement-Routed Cascade.}\nA cost-aware variant invokes NLI only below consensus threshold $\\tau$, recovering 90\\% of full-combo gain at ${\\sim}$35\\% NLI cost ($\\tau{=}0.7$; Appendix~\\ref{app:drc}).\n",

    "originalFile": "\\enlargethispage{6\\baselineskip}\n\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\n\\paragraph{Practitioner decision framework.}\n\\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict self-verification failure before deployment. $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.3$: in our experiments, no condition improves ($\\Delta \\in [0.00, -0.98]$\\,pp across four conditions; Table~\\ref{tab:independence}). When SC falls below ${\\sim}40\\%$, constraints amplify errors ($-2.83$\\,pp on Mistral-7B ProofWriter). $C_0 < 5\\%$: near-unanimous traces reduce any aggregation to majority voting by construction. Only the intermediate regime (SC $\\sim$50--60\\%, $K_\\text{eff} > 1.8$) offers marginal gain; practitioners should compute these before investing in symbolic reasoning.\n\n\\paragraph{SCB beyond SICA.}\n\\looseness=-1 SCB extends to any method extracting structured representations from a model's own outputs---self-verification \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \\citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior. Trained extractors capturing $>$12\\% of the oracle ceiling ($+29.90$\\,pp) are the most promising direction (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion}). Formal self-verification creates a \\emph{symbolic mirage}---provably sound machinery adding no information beyond majority voting---and the path forward lies in structurally independent verification, guided by $K_\\text{eff}$, BR, and $C_0$.\n",

  "new_string": "\\looseness=-1 Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. ```text
&lt;tool_use_error>String to replace not found in file.
String: \looseness=-1 Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \to \infty$ \citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:effk}
K_{\text{eff}} = \frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \cdot \kappa}.
\end{equation}
In our experiments (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\text{eff}} \approx 1.2\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. $K_{\text{eff}}$ should be interpreted as an ordinal diagnostic---ranking which conditions have genuine vote diversity---rather than as a literal effective sample size. Fleiss' $\kappa$ is a chance-corrected agreement measure, not identical to the ICC ($\rho$) in \citeauthor{kish1965survey}'s (\citeyear{kish1965survey}) original formula; we validated their monotonic relationship empirically (mean pairwise $r = 0.625$ across model families). This independence limitation motivates our investigation.&lt;/tool_use_error>
```

```text
4	\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}
5	\label{sec:condorcet}
6	
7	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \to \infty$ \citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:
8	\begin{equation}
9	\label{eq:effk}
10	K_{\text{eff}} = \frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \cdot \kappa}.
11	\end{equation}
12	In our experiments (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\text{eff}} \approx 1.2\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. $K_{\text{eff}}$ should be interpreted as an ordinal diagnostic---ranking which conditions have genuine vote diversity---rather than as a literal effective sample size. Fleiss' $\kappa$ is a chance-corrected agreement measure, not identical to the ICC ($\rho$) in \citeauthor{kish1965survey}'s (\citeyear{kish1965survey}) original formula; we validated their monotonic relationship empirically (mean pairwise $r = 0.625$ across model families). This independence limitation motivates our investigation.
13	
14	\subsection{SICA Pipeline}
15	\label{sec:pipeline}
```

This independence limitation motivates our investigation.\n\n\\subsection{SICA Pipeline}\n\\label{sec:pipeline}\n\nSymbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\n\n\\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\\{t_1, \\ldots, t_K\\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.\n\n\\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts logical constraints from each trace at $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompts}); in practice, 99.0\\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\\% using true quantifiers. The \\emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence (global average deduplication ratio: 46.0\\%); each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:maxsat}\n\\mathcal{C}^* = \\arg\\max_{\\mathcal{S} \\subseteq \\mathcal{C}} \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{S}} w_c \\quad \\text{s.t.} \\quad \\mathcal{S} \\text{ is satisfiable.}\n\\end{equation}\nZ3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\\texttt{answer\\_a} == \\texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.\n\n\\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus} = \\mathcal{C} \\setminus \\mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:score}\n\\text{score}(a) = \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^*} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a) - \\alpha \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \\arg\\max_{a} \\text{score}(a)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:degenerate}\n\\text{score}(a) \\approx \\sum_{\\substack{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^* \\\\ \\text{source}(c) \\to a}} w_c,\n\\end{equation}\nequivalent to majority voting. We show in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.\n\n\\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\\{q : |\\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\\}| / |\\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC. $\\alpha = 0.5$ is not critical (${\\pm}0.49$\\,pp across $\\alpha \\in [0, 1]$).\n\n\\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}\n\\label{sec:mirage}\n\nWe hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. This \\textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}). We quantify mirage intensity via the \\emph{Bias Ratio}:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:mirage}\n\\text{BR}(M,T) = \\frac{P(c \\text{ supports } a_M \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)}{P(c \\text{ supports } a^* \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)},\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. $\\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints project model bias rather than provide independent verification. The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). If traces are unfaithful \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. Two observations motivate the hypothesis: (O1)~BR $> 1.0$ across all tested self-extraction conditions (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (O2)~no training-free remediation reduces BR below $1.0$ (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation})---both initially observed on Mistral-7B and subsequently confirmed across nine models. These lead to a testable prediction: (P1)~structurally independent verification should break SCB (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced, colback=gray!5, colframe=gray!50, boxrule=0.3pt, left=3pt, right=3pt, top=1pt, bottom=1pt, before skip=4pt, after skip=4pt]\n\\textbf{Empirical Finding 1} (Self-Extraction Ceiling).\\label{prop:ceiling}\nFor generator $M$ with $\\text{BR}(M) > 1$ and oracle improvement $\\Delta_\\text{oracle}$, self-extracted constraints improve SC by at most $\\Delta_\\text{max}^\\text{self}$, empirically bounded by $\\Delta_\\text{oracle} / \\text{BR}(M)$.\n\\end{tcolorbox}\n\n\\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced, colback=gray!5, colframe=gray!50, boxrule=0.3pt, left=3pt, right=3pt, top=1pt, bottom=1pt, before skip=4pt, after skip=4pt]\n\\textbf{Empirical Finding 2} (Capability Gap Condition).\\label{cor:capability_gap}\nLet $G$ be a generator with SC accuracy $\\text{SC}_G$ and let $V$ be an external verifier with standalone accuracy $V_\\text{acc}$ and inter-system correlation $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$. Combo gain $\\Delta_\\text{combo} - \\Delta_\\text{SC}$ is monotonically increasing in the capability gap $(V_\\text{acc} - \\text{SC}_G)$.\n\\end{tcolorbox}\n\n\\textit{Empirical support.} From Finding~1, adding $V$ with $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$ increases $K_\\text{eff}^\\text{combo}$, but incorrect verifier votes counteract gains when $V_\\text{acc} < \\text{SC}_G$. Empirically, combo gain is monotonically ordered by the capability gap ($p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nWe also define a Conditional Correction Ratio (CCR, Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}) that summarizes these diagnostics.\n",

          " ",

  "new_string": "\\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free remediation strategies spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). We highlight four representative failure modes that illuminate the independence-accuracy trade-off:\n\n\\looseness=-1 \\emph{Debate} ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $\\Delta = -18.6$\\,pp): successfully breaks trace correlation by forcing adversarial positions, but accuracy collapses---the model cannot maintain coherent reasoning under forced disagreement. This reveals a fundamental tension: same-model diversity and quality are at odds.\n\n\\looseness=-1 \\emph{Cross-model extraction} (using a different LLM to extract constraints from the generator's traces): $\\kappa$ decreases slightly but $\\Delta \\approx 0$, because models from the same training family share systematic biases that persist across architectures (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 \\emph{Contrastive prompting} (eliciting pro/con constraints): produces imbalanced output (94 pro vs.\\ 65 con constraints on Mistral-7B FOLIO), indicating that confirmation bias transfers from answer generation to constraint generation.\n\n\\looseness=-1 \\emph{Oracle ceiling}: gold-annotated constraints yield $+29.90$\\,pp (Mistral-7B FOLIO), proving the MAX-SAT mechanism is sound. Self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling, precisely localizing the bottleneck to extraction quality rather than aggregation.\n\n\\looseness=-1 All non-debate strategies cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling across three families (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). No individual strategy achieves significance after correction (Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}). Trained extractors remain the most promising direction for closing the oracle gap."
}

    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We test the three predictions of the symbolic mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Table~\\ref{tab:independence} (Appendix) reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$ as an ordinal diagnostic: higher $K_{\\text{eff}}$ indicates greater vote diversity, though it should not be interpreted as a literal effective sample size (\\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}). Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades). On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. A shuffle control reveals this bias is \\emph{structural}: randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts yields $\\text{BR}_\\text{null} \\approx \\text{BR}_\\text{obs}$ ($p > 0.7$), indicating that BR reflects the trace-count distribution rather than extraction-specific error. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\\% of problems on Mistral-7B FOLIO (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming that the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}): 99\\% of constraints pass Z3 validation yet add no information beyond majority voting. This vacuity is $\\alpha$-insensitive: agreement $\\geq$99\\% across $\\alpha \\in \\{0.1, \\ldots, 0.9\\}$ for both high- and moderate-BR conditions (Table~\\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 This pattern generalizes: $4.97\\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen-14B), $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA), all $> 1.0$ with 95\\% bootstrap CIs (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Cross-model extraction confirms SCB universality across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}). Ext.: $^a$self-extraction; $^b$cross-model (Mistral-7B extracts FOL from target traces). $^*$Interim ($n = 95/204$). All models exhibit $\\text{BR} > 1$; frontier models show relatively lower BR ($1.66$--$2.79\\times$ for o3/Gemini), suggesting SCB severity may decrease with capability, though confounded by extraction method ($^a$ vs.\\ $^b$).}\n\\label{tab:frontier-scb}\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Ext. & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & BR & $\\kappa_{\\text{proc}}$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & $^a$ & 55.88 & $+$3.43 & 4.97 & 0.690 \\\\\nLLaMA-8B & $^a$ & \\textrm{---} & \\textrm{---} & 2.88 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\nQwen-14B & $^a$ & 75.49 & $+$0.25 & 3.93 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nR1-Distill-8B & $^b$ & 74.51 & $+$2.45 & 2.92 & 0.248 \\\\\ngpt-4o & $^a$ & 73.91 & $-$1.86 & 2.94 & 0.790 \\\\\ngpt-4.1 & $^b$ & 82.84 & $-$1.47 & 2.70 & 0.792 \\\\\ngpt-5.5 & $^b$ & 84.31 & $+$0.49 & 3.48 & 0.563 \\\\\no3 & $^b$ & 82.65 & 0.00 & 1.66 & 0.596 \\\\\nGemini-2.5-Pro$^*$ & $^b$ & 87.37 & $-$1.05 & 2.79 & 0.790 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Two controls rule out answer leakage. BR is uniform across early, mid, and late reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$); high-consensus errors amplify to $\\text{BR} = 18.8\\times$ versus $2.7\\times$ for low-consensus ($p < 0.001$). {
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "804a1fb6-ff30-463a-9d79-9ef8b48ce0e4",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "bc0bb3f5-0060-4d2e-80aa-f5d4e9069f03",
  "type": "user",
  "uuid": "d46075fd-67a1-4008-bdf5-b6e21faff736",
  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/method.tex",
    "oldString": "Following \\citet{ladha1992correlated} and \\citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size $K_{\\text{eff}} = K / [1 + (K{-}1) \\cdot \\kappa]$ using Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation.",
    "newString": "Following \\citet{ladha1992correlated} and \\citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:effk}\nK_{\\text{eff}} = K \\,/\\, [1 + (K{-}1) \\cdot \\kappa].\n\\end{equation}",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Background \\& Setup}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}\n\\label{sec:condorcet}\n\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees majority convergence under independent voters \\citep{austensmith1996}, but LLM traces violate this independence. Following \\citet{ladha1992correlated} and \\citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size $K_{\\text{eff}} = K / [1 + (K{-}1) \\cdot \\kappa]$ using Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation. In our experiments (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ yields $K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2$--$3.6$ for $K{=}12$, serving as an ordinal diagnostic of vote diversity rather than a literal effective sample size. This independence limitation motivates our investigation.\n\n\\subsection{SICA Pipeline}\n\\label{sec:pipeline}\n\nSymbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\n\n\\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\\{t_1, \\ldots, t_K\\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.\n\n\\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts logical constraints from each trace at $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompts}); in practice, 99.0\\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\\% using true quantifiers. The \\emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence (global average deduplication ratio: 46.0\\%); each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:maxsat}\n\\mathcal{C}^* = \\arg\\max_{\\mathcal{S} \\subseteq \\mathcal{C}} \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{S}} w_c \\quad \\text{s.t.} \\quad \\mathcal{S} \\text{ is satisfiable.}\n\\end{equation}\nZ3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\\texttt{answer\\_a} == \\texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.\n\n\\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus} = \\mathcal{C} \\setminus \\mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:score}\n\\text{score}(a) = \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^*} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a) - \\alpha \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \\arg\\max_{a} \\text{score}(a)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:degenerate}\n\\text{score}(a) \\approx \\sum_{\\substack{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^* \\\\ \\text{source}(c) \\to a}} w_c,\n\\end{equation}\nequivalent to majority voting. We show in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.\n\n\\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\\{q : |\\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\\}| / |\\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC. $\\alpha = 0.5$ is not critical (${\\pm}0.49$\\,pp across $\\alpha \\in [0, 1]$).\n\n\\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}\n\\label{sec:mirage}\n\nWe hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. This \\textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}). We quantify mirage intensity via the \\emph{Bias Ratio}:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:mirage}\n\\text{BR}(M,T) = \\frac{P(c \\text{ supports } a_M \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)}{P(c \\text{ supports } a^* \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)},\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. $\\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints project model bias rather than provide independent verification. The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). If traces are unfaithful \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. Two observations motivate the hypothesis: (O1)~BR $> 1.0$ across all tested self-extraction conditions (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (O2)~no training-free remediation reduces BR below $1.0$ (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation})---both initially observed on Mistral-7B and subsequently confirmed across nine models. These lead to a testable prediction: (P1)~structurally independent verification should break SCB (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced, colback=gray!5, colframe=gray!50, boxrule=0.3pt, left=3pt, right=3pt, top=1pt, bottom=1pt, before skip=4pt, after skip=4pt]\n\\textbf{Empirical Finding 1} (Self-Extraction Ceiling).\\label{prop:ceiling}\nFor generator $M$ with $\\text{BR}(M) > 1$ and oracle improvement $\\Delta_\\text{oracle}$, self-extracted constraints improve SC by at most $\\Delta_\\text{max}^\\text{self}$, empirically bounded by $\\Delta_\\text{oracle} / \\text{BR}(M)$.\n\\end{tcolorbox}\n\n\\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced, colback=gray!5, colframe=gray!50, boxrule=0.3pt, left=3pt, right=3pt, top=1pt, bottom=1pt, before skip=4pt, after skip=4pt]\n\\textbf{Empirical Finding 2} (Capability Gap Condition).\\label{cor:capability_gap}\nLet $G$ be a generator with SC accuracy $\\text{SC}_G$ and let $V$ be an external verifier with standalone accuracy $V_\\text{acc}$ and inter-system correlation $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$. Combo gain $\\Delta_\\text{combo} - \\Delta_\\text{SC}$ is monotonically increasing in the capability gap $(V_\\text{acc} - \\text{SC}_G)$.\n\\end{tcolorbox}\n\n\\textit{Empirical support.} From Finding~1, adding $V$ with $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$ increases $K_\\text{eff}^\\text{combo}$, but incorrect verifier votes counteract gains when $V_\\text{acc} < \\text{SC}_G$. Empirically, combo gain is monotonically ordered by the capability gap ($p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nWe also define a Conditional Correction Ratio (CCR, Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}) that summarizes these diagnostics.\n",

Following \\citet{ladha1992correlated} and \\citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size $K_{\\text{eff}} = K / [1 + (K{-}1) \\cdot \\kappa]$ using Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation.     "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We test the three predictions of the symbolic mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Table~\\ref{tab:independence} (Appendix) reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$ as an ordinal diagnostic: higher $K_{\\text{eff}}$ indicates greater vote diversity, though it should not be interpreted as a literal effective sample size (\\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}). Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades). All four model families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. A shuffle control reveals this bias is \\emph{structural}: randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts yields $\\text{BR}_\\text{null} \\approx \\text{BR}_\\text{obs}$ ($p > 0.7$), indicating that BR reflects the trace-count distribution rather than extraction-specific error. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\\% of problems on Mistral-7B FOLIO (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming that the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}): 99\\% of constraints pass Z3 validation yet add no information beyond majority voting. This vacuity is $\\alpha$-insensitive: agreement $\\geq$99\\% across $\\alpha \\in \\{0.1, \\ldots, 0.9\\}$ for both high- and moderate-BR conditions (Table~\\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 This pattern generalizes: $4.97\\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen-14B), $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA), all $> 1.0$ with 95\\% bootstrap CIs (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Cross-model extraction confirms SCB universality across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}). BR $> 1$ further holds across all eight cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix on FOLIO and ProofWriter (range $1.94$--$2.70$; Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Symbolic confirmation bias across nine models on FOLIO ($n = 204$). BR: mean per-problem bias ratio. $\\kappa_{\\text{proc}}$: process-level cosine similarity. Ext.: $^a$self-extraction; $^b$cross-model (Mistral-7B extracts FOL from target traces). $^*$Interim ($n = 95/204$). All models exhibit $\\text{BR} > 1$; frontier models show relatively lower BR ($1.66$--$2.79\\times$ for o3/Gemini), suggesting SCB severity may decrease with capability, though confounded by extraction method ($^a$ vs.\\ $^b$).}\n\\label{tab:frontier-scb}\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Ext. & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & BR & $\\kappa_{\\text{proc}}$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & $^a$ & 55.88 & $+$3.43 & 4.97 & 0.690 \\\\\nLLaMA-8B & $^a$ & \\textrm{---} & \\textrm{---} & 2.88 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\nQwen-14B & $^a$ & 75.49 & $+$0.25 & 3.93 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nR1-Distill-8B & $^b$ & 74.51 & $+$2.45 & 2.92 & 0.248 \\\\\ngpt-4o & $^a$ & 73.91 & $-$1.86 & 2.94 & 0.790 \\\\\ngpt-4.1 & $^b$ & 82.84 & $-$1.47 & 2.70 & 0.792 \\\\\ngpt-5.5 & $^b$ & 84.31 & $+$0.49 & 3.48 & 0.563 \\\\\no3 & $^b$ & 82.65 & 0.00 & 1.66 & 0.596 \\\\\nGemini-2.5-Pro$^*$ & $^b$ & 87.37 & $-$1.05 & 2.79 & 0.790 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Two controls rule out answer leakage. BR is uniform across early, mid, and late reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$); high-consensus errors amplify to $\\text{BR} = 18.8\\times$ versus $2.7\\times$ for low-consensus ($p < 0.001$). Removing the answer sentence from traces before extraction yields no BR decrease (Wilcoxon $p \\geq 0.12$) despite significant accuracy loss (McNemar $p < 0.013$). SCB reflects a distributional prior embedded throughout reasoning, not answer contamination.\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We quantify reasoning-process overlap via ProcessSim, the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace vectors (Eq.~\\ref{eq:process-sim}, Appendix~\\ref{app:process-gradient}). ProcessSim captures lexical overlap; deeper structure measures may reveal additional patterns, so we treat it as supporting rather than primary evidence. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B). Under cross-model extraction, SICA gain decreases monotonically with ProcessSim ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.008$; Table~\\ref{tab:process-gradient}), modulated by self-bias severity.\n\n\nCCR analysis confirms the same pattern: cross-architecture NLI yields $\\text{CCR} > 0$ while same-model SICA yields $\\text{CCR} < 0$, with CCR correlating with combo gain ($\\rho = 0.84$, $p = 0.014$; Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}).\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free remediation strategies spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). We highlight four representative failure modes that illuminate the independence-accuracy trade-off:\n\n\\looseness=-1 \\emph{Debate} ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $\\Delta = -18.6$\\,pp): successfully breaks trace correlation by forcing adversarial positions, but accuracy collapses---the model cannot maintain coherent reasoning under forced disagreement. This reveals a fundamental tension: same-model diversity and quality are at odds.\n\n\\looseness=-1 \\emph{Cross-model extraction} (using a different LLM to extract constraints from the generator's traces): $\\kappa$ decreases slightly but $\\Delta \\approx 0$, because models from the same training family share systematic biases that persist across architectures (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 \\emph{Contrastive prompting} (eliciting pro/con constraints): produces imbalanced output (94 pro vs.\\ 65 con constraints on Mistral-7B FOLIO), indicating that confirmation bias transfers from answer generation to constraint generation.\n\n\\looseness=-1 \\emph{Oracle ceiling}: gold-annotated constraints yield $+29.90$\\,pp (Mistral-7B FOLIO), proving the MAX-SAT mechanism is sound. Self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling, precisely localizing the bottleneck to extraction quality rather than aggregation.\n\n\\looseness=-1 All non-debate strategies cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling across three families (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). No individual strategy achieves significance after correction (Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}). Trained extractors remain the most promising direction for closing the oracle gap.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_interventions_compact.pdf}\n\\caption{Twenty-three of 27 training-free strategies across four categories (four additional strategies tested but omitted for clarity). All produce neutral-to-negative $\\Delta$ vs.\\ SC. Grey band: $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis: verification.}\n\\looseness=-1 The observations and prediction of \\S\\ref{sec:mirage} are confirmed. Observations O1--O2, initially discovered on Mistral-7B, generalize: BR $> 1$ across three self-extraction models, six cross-model pairs, and all eight cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); 27 training-free strategies fail to reduce BR below 1.0 (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). Prediction P1 is validated: cross-architecture NLI escapes the mirage when the verifier exceeds the generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n",
