```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}
5	SC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early; \citet{byerly2026selfconsistency} further analyze SC's limitations in mathematical reasoning. Alternative aggregation strategies beyond majority voting \citep{ai2025beyondmajority} have been proposed but do not address the underlying correlation problem. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
6	
7	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
8	We distinguish three paradigms. \emph{Specification-generation} (Logic-LM \citep{pan2023logiclm}, LINC \citep{olausson2023linc}, SatLM \citep{ye2024satlm}) translates problem descriptions into formal specifications before any answer is generated; LINC documents ${\sim}$40\% translation errors even at this level. \emph{Program-generation} \citep{gao2023pal,chen2023program,lyu2023faithful} produces executable code from problem statements. Both paradigms access the problem before generating candidate answers. \emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc on reasoning traces that already contain candidates, inheriting confirmation bias (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). SatLM provides a natural control: it succeeds because problem-level formalization precedes answer generation, whereas trace-level extraction cannot avoid contamination (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). Recent extensions \citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku} introduce capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization degrades with complexity; \citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\,pp FOLIO, $+17.17$\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction (\S\ref{sec:oracle}).
9	
10	\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}
11	Scaling test-time computation through additional traces \citep{snell2025scaling} or structured search \citep{yao2023tree} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to correlated same-model voting: QwQ-32B produces $\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-verification \citep{weng2023selfverification}, chain-of-verification \citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification}, self-refinement \citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, tool-augmented critique \citep{gou2024critic}, and formal CoT verification \citep{zhu2025vericot} report gains, but none systematically tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers. We find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; \citet{valmeekam2023llms} further show fundamental planning limitations that compound self-verification failures. Our diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\kappa$) predict when trace-aggregation will fail.
12	
13	\paragraph{CJT and positioning.}
14	CJT \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size. Classical ensemble diversity theory \citep{kuncheva2003measures, brown2005diversity} formalizes this intuition: diversity measures (e.g., $\kappa$-statistic, disagreement) predict ensemble gain. Our $K_\text{eff}$ adapts Fleiss' $\kappa$ for this purpose. \citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. \citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs. Concurrent work by \citet{kuai2026independent} proposes BEI and CIG to audit cross-model behavioral dependencies; our process-$\kappa$ and $K_\text{eff}$ are complementary, operating at the reasoning-process level within same-model traces and identifying a specific causal mechanism (SCB) that behavioral metrics miss.
15	Prior self-correction studies \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute quantitative diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_D$). SCB is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \citep{turpin2024language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}: the latter concern whether stated reasoning reflects actual computation, while SCB concerns whether \emph{self-extracted formal constraints} mirror the model's answer distribution. Formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$)---invisible to prior work on natural-language self-correction.
```

We find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; \\citet{valmeekam2023llms} further show fundamental planning limitations that compound self-verification failures. Our diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\\kappa$) predict when trace-aggregation will fail.\n\n\\paragraph{CJT and positioning.}\nCJT \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size. Classical ensemble diversity theory \\citep{kuncheva2003measures, brown2005diversity} formalizes this intuition: diversity measures (e.g., $\\kappa$-statistic, disagreement) predict ensemble gain. Our $K_\\text{eff}$ adapts Fleiss' $\\kappa$ for this purpose. \\citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. \\citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs. Concurrent work by \\citet{kuai2026independent} proposes BEI and CIG to audit cross-model behavioral dependencies; our process-$\\kappa$ and $K_\\text{eff}$ are complementary, operating at the reasoning-process level within same-model traces and identifying a specific causal mechanism (SCB) that behavioral metrics miss.\nPrior self-correction studies \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute quantitative diagnostics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_D$). SCB is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \\citep{turpin2024language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}: the latter concern whether stated reasoning reflects actual computation, while SCB concerns whether \\emph{self-extracted formal constraints} mirror the model's answer distribution. Formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$)---invisible to prior work on natural-language self-correction.",

        "lines": [
          " We distinguish three paradigms. \\emph{Specification-generation} (Logic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm}, LINC \\citep{olausson2023linc}, SatLM \\citep{ye2024satlm}) translates problem descriptions into formal specifications before any answer is generated; LINC documents ${\\sim}$40\\% translation errors even at this level. \\emph{Program-generation} \\citep{gao2023pal,chen2023program,lyu2023faithful} produces executable code from problem statements. Both paradigms access the problem before generating candidate answers. \\emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc on reasoning traces that already contain candidates, inheriting confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). SatLM provides a natural control: it succeeds because problem-level formalization precedes answer generation, whereas trace-level extraction cannot avoid contamination (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). Recent extensions \\citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku} introduce capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization degrades with complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\\,pp FOLIO, $+17.17$\\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).",

Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \\citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to correlated same-model voting: QwQ-32B produces $\\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-verification \\citep{weng2023selfverification}, chain-of-verification \\citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, tool-augmented critique \\citep{gou2024critic}, and formal CoT verification \\citep{zhu2025vericot} report gains, but none systematically tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers. We find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).     "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:exp-setup}\n\nWe evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models, three reasoning domains, and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), with diagnostic analyses of confirmation bias and process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}).\n\n\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). We additionally evaluate SCB universality via cross-model extraction on five frontier models (gpt-4o, gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}), where Mistral-7B extracts constraints from the target model's traces since closed-source APIs do not expose internals suitable for self-extraction.\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\\%$).\n\n\\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s. McNemar exact test ($\\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples) for $n \\geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\\% of overhead.\n\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\\label{sec:main-results}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all conditions sorted by SC baseline, revealing three observation-driven accuracy regimes.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{SICA vs.\\ SC across all conditions (sorted by SC). No non-degenerate condition shows a significant gain after BH correction ($m{=}16$). $\\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). Symbols: $^b$gold ICL; $\\dagger$\\,$n < 100$; \\textbf{bold}: $p < 0.05$; {*}\\,uncorrected $p < 0.05$ (BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$); {***}\\,oracle $p < 0.001$; $^{3s}$\\,3-seed; $^{4s}$\\,4-seed Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter). The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted $\\Delta$ ($+3.43$\\,pp) captures only 11.5\\% of its oracle ceiling. For Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter, the gap is wider still: self-extraction degrades accuracy ($-2.50$\\,pp) against an oracle ceiling of $+17.17$\\,pp, indicating that stronger models do not self-extract more effectively.\n\n\n",

112	\paragraph{Intermediate-SC region.} Mistral-7B on FOLIO (SC $\sim$50--60\%) shows positive directional trends in 2 of 4 seeds: $\Delta = +3.43$\,pp ($p = 0.092$) and $+2.94$\,pp ($p = 0.070$); the remaining seeds show null or negative results. No seed reaches $p < 0.05$; the 4-seed mean is $+1.47$\,pp. The default-seed and seed-456 runs share identical SC baselines (55.88\%) yet diverge ($+3.43$ vs.\ $0.00$\,pp), suggesting that seed-level constraint quality, not SC level alone, influences the effect.
113	
114	\paragraph{Type A (SC $\geq$ 60\%).} All nine Type~A conditions produce no significant positive effects. Even in-context learning with gold logical examples yields $\Delta = +0.50$\,pp (n.s.). Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter shows significant degradation ($\Delta = -2.50$\,pp, $p = 0.011$).

114	\paragraph{Type A (SC $\geq$ 60\%).} All nine Type~A conditions produce no significant positive effects. Even in-context learning with gold logical examples yields $\Delta = +0.50$\,pp (n.s.). Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter shows significant degradation ($\Delta = -2.50$\,pp, $p = 0.011$).
115	
116	\paragraph{Type C (SC $\leq$ 40\%).} Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($n = 600$, SC $= 39.33\%$) shows $\Delta = -2.83$\,pp ($p = 0.014$): SICA \emph{significantly hurts} by formalizing incorrect reasoning. When $C_0 < 5\%$, nearly all traces agree and SICA reduces to SC by construction.
117	
118	Four supporting analyses confirm the mechanism: constraint ablation shows MAX-SAT and count-only agree on 94.4\% of predictions ($n = 804$); 91.8\% of constraints are discriminative but mirror the majority; effects concentrate in high-entropy problems ($+12.8$\,pp) with direction determined by accuracy regime; and hyperparameter peaks coincide with low SC baselines (Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}).
119	
120	\begin{figure}[t]
121	\centering
122	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{f2_regime_scatter}

57	\label{sec:interventions}
58	
59	\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free remediation strategies spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}). We highlight four representative failure modes that illuminate the independence-accuracy trade-off:
60	
61	\looseness=-1 \emph{Debate} ($\kappa = 0.15$, $\Delta = -18.6$\,pp): successfully breaks trace correlation by forcing adversarial positions, but accuracy collapses---the model cannot maintain coherent reasoning under forced disagreement. This reveals a fundamental tension: same-model diversity and quality are at odds.
62	
63	\looseness=-1 \emph{Cross-model extraction} (using a different LLM to extract constraints from the generator's traces): $\kappa$ decreases slightly but $\Delta \approx 0$, because models from the same training family share systematic biases that persist across architectures (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).
64	
65	\looseness=-1 \emph{Contrastive prompting} (eliciting pro/con constraints): produces imbalanced output (94 pro vs.\ 65 con constraints on Mistral-7B FOLIO), indicating that confirmation bias transfers from answer generation to constraint generation.
66	
67	\looseness=-1 \emph{Oracle ceiling}: gold-annotated constraints yield $+29.90$\,pp (Mistral-7B FOLIO), proving the MAX-SAT mechanism is sound. Self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of this ceiling, precisely localizing the bottleneck to extraction quality rather than aggregation.
68	
69	\looseness=-1 All non-debate strategies cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling across three families (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). No individual strategy achieves significance after correction (Appendix~\ref{app:intervention-individual}). Trained extractors remain the most promising direction for closing the oracle gap.
70	
71	\begin{figure}[t]
72	\centering
73	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_interventions_compact.pdf}
74	\caption{Twenty-three of 27 training-free strategies across four categories (four additional strategies tested but omitted for clarity). All produce neutral-to-negative $\Delta$ vs.\ SC. Grey band: $\pm 1$\,pp noise zone.}
75	\label{fig:interventions-summary}
76	\end{figure}
```

    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We test the three predictions of the symbolic mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Table~\\ref{tab:independence} (Appendix) reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$ as an ordinal diagnostic: higher $K_{\\text{eff}}$ indicates greater vote diversity, though it should not be interpreted as a literal effective sample size (\\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}). Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades). All four model families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. A shuffle control reveals this bias is \\emph{structural}: randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts yields $\\text{BR}_\\text{null} \\approx \\text{BR}_\\text{obs}$ ($p > 0.7$), indicating that BR reflects the trace-count distribution rather than extraction-specific error. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\\% of problems on Mistral-7B FOLIO (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming that the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}): 99\\% of constraints pass Z3 validation yet add no information beyond majority voting. This vacuity is $\\alpha$-insensitive: agreement $\\geq$99\\% across $\\alpha \\in \\{0.1, \\ldots, 0.9\\}$ for both high- and moderate-BR conditions (Table~\\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 This pattern generalizes: $4.97\\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen-14B), $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA), all $> 1.0$ with 95\\% bootstrap CIs (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Cross-model extraction confirms SCB universality across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}). BR $> 1$ further holds across all eight cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix on FOLIO and ProofWriter (range $1.94$--$2.70$; Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Symbolic confirmation bias across nine models on FOLIO ($n = 204$). BR: mean per-problem bias ratio. $n_\\text{err}$: number of incorrect instances ($n \\times (1 - \\text{SC}/100)$) on which BR is computed. $\\kappa_{\\text{proc}}$: process-level cosine similarity. Ext.: $^a$self-extraction; $^b$cross-model (Mistral-7B extracts FOL from target traces). $^*$Interim ($n = 95/204$). All models exhibit $\\text{BR} > 1$; BR estimates for high-accuracy models ($n_\\text{err} < 40$) have wider confidence intervals; Gemini-2.5-Pro ($n_\\text{err} \\approx 12$) should be interpreted with particular caution.}\n\\label{tab:frontier-scb}\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Ext. & SC (\\%) & $n_\\text{err}$ & BR & $\\kappa_{\\text{proc}}$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & $^a$ & 55.88 & 90 & 4.97 & 0.690 \\\\\nLLaMA-8B & $^a$ & \\textrm{---} & \\textrm{---} & 2.88 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\nQwen-14B & $^a$ & 75.49 & 50 & 3.93 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nR1-Distill-8B & $^b$ & 74.51 & 52 & 2.92 & 0.248 \\\\\ngpt-4o & $^a$ & 73.91 & 53 & 2.94 & 0.790 \\\\\ngpt-4.1 & $^b$ & 82.84 & 35 & 2.70 & 0.792 \\\\\ngpt-5.5 & $^b$ & 84.31 & 32 & 3.48 & 0.563 \\\\\no3 & $^b$ & 82.65 & 35 & 1.66 & 0.596 \\\\\nGemini-2.5-Pro$^*$ & $^b$ & 87.37 & 12 & 2.79 & 0.790 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Two controls rule out answer leakage. BR is uniform across early, mid, and late reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$); high-consensus errors amplify to $\\text{BR} = 18.8\\times$ versus $2.7\\times$ for low-consensus ($p < 0.001$). Removing the answer sentence from traces before extraction yields no BR decrease (Wilcoxon $p \\geq 0.12$) despite significant accuracy loss (McNemar $p < 0.013$). SCB reflects a distributional prior embedded throughout reasoning, not answer contamination.\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We quantify reasoning-process overlap via ProcessSim, the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace vectors (Eq.~\\ref{eq:process-sim}, Appendix~\\ref{app:process-gradient}). ProcessSim captures lexical overlap; deeper structure measures may reveal additional patterns, so we treat it as supporting rather than primary evidence. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B). Under cross-model extraction, SICA gain decreases monotonically with ProcessSim ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.008$; Table~\\ref{tab:process-gradient}), modulated by self-bias severity.\n\n\nCCR analysis confirms the same pattern: cross-architecture NLI yields $\\text{CCR} > 0$ while same-model SICA yields $\\text{CCR} < 0$, with CCR correlating with combo gain ($\\rho = 0.84$, $p = 0.014$; Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}).\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free remediation strategies spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). We highlight four representative failure modes that illuminate the independence-accuracy trade-off:\n\n\\looseness=-1 \\emph{Debate} ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $\\Delta = -18.6$\\,pp): successfully breaks trace correlation by forcing adversarial positions, but accuracy collapses---the model cannot maintain coherent reasoning under forced disagreement. This reveals a fundamental tension: same-model diversity and quality are at odds.\n\n\\looseness=-1 \\emph{Cross-model extraction} (using a different LLM to extract constraints from the generator's traces): $\\kappa$ decreases slightly but $\\Delta \\approx 0$, because models from the same training family share systematic biases that persist across architectures (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 \\emph{Contrastive prompting} (eliciting pro/con constraints): produces imbalanced output (94 pro vs.\\ 65 con constraints on Mistral-7B FOLIO), indicating that confirmation bias transfers from answer generation to constraint generation.\n\n\\looseness=-1 \\emph{Oracle ceiling}: gold-annotated constraints yield $+29.90$\\,pp (Mistral-7B FOLIO), proving the MAX-SAT mechanism is sound. Self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling, precisely localizing the bottleneck to extraction quality rather than aggregation.\n\n\\looseness=-1 All non-debate strategies cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling across three families (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). No individual strategy achieves significance after correction (Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}). Trained extractors remain the most promising direction for closing the oracle gap.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_interventions_compact.pdf}\n\\caption{Twenty-three of 27 training-free strategies across four categories (four additional strategies tested but omitted for clarity). All produce neutral-to-negative $\\Delta$ vs.\\ SC. Grey band: $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis: verification.}\n\\looseness=-1 The observations and prediction of \\S\\ref{sec:mirage} are confirmed. Observations O1--O2, initially discovered on Mistral-7B, generalize: BR $> 1$ across three self-extraction models, six cross-model pairs, and all eight cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); 27 training-free strategies fail to reduce BR below 1.0 (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). Prediction P1 is validated: cross-architecture NLI escapes the mirage when the verifier exceeds the generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n",

    "originalFile": "\\enlargethispage{6\\baselineskip}\n\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\n\\paragraph{Practitioner decision framework.}\n\\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict self-verification failure before deployment. $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.3$: in our experiments, no condition improves ($\\Delta \\in [0.00, -0.98]$\\,pp across four conditions; Table~\\ref{tab:independence}). When SC falls below ${\\sim}40\\%$, constraints amplify errors ($-2.83$\\,pp on Mistral-7B ProofWriter). $C_0 < 5\\%$: near-unanimous traces reduce any aggregation to majority voting by construction. Only the intermediate regime (SC $\\sim$50--60\\%, $K_\\text{eff} > 1.8$) offers marginal gain; practitioners should compute these before investing in symbolic reasoning.\n\n\\paragraph{SCB beyond SICA.}\n\\looseness=-1 SCB extends to any method extracting structured representations from a model's own outputs---self-verification \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \\citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior. Our debate control ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp) confirms that independence and quality are at odds under same-model constraints.\n\n\\paragraph{Design principles for escaping SCB.}\n\\looseness=-1 Based on this proof-of-concept, we identify three conditions that appear necessary for escaping SCB: (i)~\\emph{structural independence}---no shared architecture or training data; (ii)~\\emph{positive capability gap}---the verifier's adjusted contribution exceeds the generator's SC; (iii)~\\emph{task-verifier alignment}---the verification paradigm matches task structure. These are consistent with why 27 same-model interventions fail ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$) while a 406M encoder succeeds ($+11.00$\\,pp), though validation beyond propositional-logic NLI is needed. Trained extractors capturing $>$12\\% of the oracle ceiling ($+29.90$\\,pp) are the most promising direction (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion}). Formal self-verification creates a \\emph{symbolic mirage}---provably sound machinery adding no information beyond majority voting---and the path forward lies in structurally independent verification, guided by $K_\\text{eff}$, BR, and $C_0$.\n",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Xvu6fVUugZMVeqgQ76Ps1B`</summary>

```text
1	\enlargethispage{8\baselineskip}
2	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
3	\label{sec:discussion}
4	\label{sec:conclusion}
5	
6	\paragraph{Practitioner decision framework.}
7	\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict self-verification failure before deployment. $K_\text{eff} \leq 1.3$: in our experiments, no condition improves ($\Delta \in [0.00, -0.98]$\,pp across four conditions; Table~\ref{tab:independence}). When SC falls below ${\sim}40\%$, constraints amplify errors ($-2.83$\,pp on Mistral-7B ProofWriter). $C_0 < 5\%$: near-unanimous traces reduce any aggregation to majority voting by construction. Only the intermediate regime (SC $\sim$50--60\%, $K_\text{eff} > 1.8$) offers marginal gain; practitioners should compute these before investing in symbolic reasoning.
8	
9	\paragraph{SCB beyond SICA.}
10	\looseness=-1 SCB extends to any method extracting structured representations from a model's own outputs---self-verification \citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior. Our debate control ($\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\,pp) confirms that independence and quality are at odds under same-model constraints.
11	
12	\paragraph{Design principles for escaping SCB.}
13	\looseness=-1 Based on this proof-of-concept, we identify three conditions that appear necessary for escaping SCB: (i)~\emph{structural independence}---no shared architecture or training data; (ii)~\emph{positive capability gap}---the verifier's adjusted contribution exceeds the generator's SC; (iii)~\emph{task-verifier alignment}---the verification paradigm matches task structure. These are consistent with why 27 same-model interventions fail ($\kappa \geq 0.40$) while a 406M encoder succeeds ($+11.00$\,pp), though validation beyond propositional-logic NLI is needed. Trained extractors capturing $>$12\% of the oracle ceiling ($+29.90$\,pp) are the most promising direction (Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}). Formal self-verification creates a \emph{symbolic mirage}---provably sound machinery adding no information beyond majority voting---and the path forward lies in structurally independent verification, guided by $K_\text{eff}$, BR, and $C_0$.
14	
```

</details>

   - Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis: restructured from "three predictions" to "two observations (O1, O2) + one testable prediction (P1)"
   - Four diagnostics: K_eff, BR, C₀, ProcessSim

 notation for 3-seed confirmatory, `$^{4s}

These lead to a testable prediction: (P1)~structurally independent verification should break SCB"

   - **analysis.tex** — Multiple changes:
     - §5.4 CCR subsection replaced with single sentence: "CCR analysis confirms the same pattern: cross-architecture NLI yields CCR > 0 while same-model SICA yields CCR < 0, with CCR correlating with combo gain (ρ = 0.84, p = 0.014; Appendix)"

   - **discussion.tex** — Final state:
     ```latex
     \enlargethispage{8\baselineskip}
     \section{Discussion and Conclusion}
     \label{sec:discussion}
     \label{sec:conclusion}
     
     \paragraph{Practitioner decision framework.}
     \looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict self-verification failure: $K_\text{eff} \leq 1.3$ (no condition improves; Table~\ref{tab:independence}), SC $< 40\%$ (constraints amplify errors; $-2.83$\,pp), and $C_0 < 5\%$ (unanimous traces reduce aggregation to majority voting). Only the intermediate regime (SC $\sim$50--60\%, $K_\text{eff} > 1.8$) offers marginal gain; practitioners should compute these before investing in symbolic reasoning.
     
     \paragraph{SCB beyond SICA.}
     \looseness=-1 SCB extends to any method extracting structured representations from a model's own outputs---self-verification \citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior. Our debate control ($\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\,pp) confirms that independence and quality are at odds under same-model constraints.
     
     \paragraph{Design principles for escaping SCB.}
     \looseness=-1 Based on this proof-of-concept, we identify three conditions that appear necessary: (i)~structural independence; (ii)~positive capability gap; (iii)~task-verifier alignment. These explain why 27 same-model interventions fail ($\kappa \geq 0.40$) while a 406M encoder succeeds ($+11.00$\,pp), though validation beyond propositional-logic NLI is needed. Formal self-verification creates a symbolic mirage---provably sound machinery adding no information beyond majority voting---and the path forward lies in structurally independent verification, guided by $K_\text{eff}$, BR, and $C_0$ (Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}).
     ```

   - **related_work.tex** — Added SETS/MAV placeholder citations:

        "content": "\\enlargethispage{8\\baselineskip}\n\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\n\\paragraph{Practitioner decision framework.}\n\\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict self-verification failure: $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.3$ (no condition improves; Table~\\ref{tab:independence}), SC $< 40\\%$ (constraints amplify errors; $-2.83$\\,pp), and $C_0 < 5\\%$ (unanimous traces reduce aggregation to majority voting). Our debate control ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp) confirms that independence and quality are at odds under same-model constraints.\n\n\\paragraph{Design principles for escaping SCB.}\n\\looseness=-1 Based on this proof-of-concept, we identify three conditions that appear necessary: (i)~\\emph{structural independence}---no shared architecture or training data; (ii)~\\emph{positive capability gap}---verifier accuracy exceeds the generator's SC; (iii)~\\emph{task-verifier alignment}---the verification paradigm matches task structure. These explain why 27 same-model interventions fail ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$) while a 406M encoder succeeds ($+11.00$\\,pp), though validation beyond propositional-logic NLI is needed. Formal self-verification creates a \\emph{symbolic mirage}---provably sound machinery adding no information beyond majority voting---and the path forward lies in structurally independent verification, guided by $K_\\text{eff}$, BR, and $C_0$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion}).\n",
        "numLines": 14,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 14
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/discussion.tex"
  },

        "content": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We test the three predictions of the symbolic mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Table~\\ref{tab:independence} (Appendix) reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$ as an ordinal diagnostic: higher $K_{\\text{eff}}$ indicates greater vote diversity, though it should not be interpreted as a literal effective sample size (\\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}). Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades). All four model families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. A shuffle control reveals this bias is \\emph{structural}: randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts yields $\\text{BR}_\\text{null} \\approx \\text{BR}_\\text{obs}$ ($p > 0.7$), indicating that BR reflects the trace-count distribution rather than extraction-specific error. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\\% of problems on Mistral-7B FOLIO (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming that the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}): 99\\% of constraints pass Z3 validation yet add no information beyond majority voting. This vacuity is $\\alpha$-insensitive: agreement $\\geq$99\\% across $\\alpha \\in \\{0.1, \\ldots, 0.9\\}$ for both high- and moderate-BR conditions (Table~\\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 This pattern generalizes: $4.97\\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen-14B), $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA), all $> 1.0$ with 95\\% bootstrap CIs (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Cross-model extraction confirms SCB universality across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}). BR $> 1$ further holds across all eight cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix on FOLIO and ProofWriter (range $1.94$--$2.70$; Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Symbolic confirmation bias across nine models on FOLIO ($n = 204$). BR: mean per-problem bias ratio. $n_\\text{err}$: number of incorrect instances ($n \\times (1 - \\text{SC}/100)$) on which BR is computed. $\\kappa_{\\text{proc}}$: process-level cosine similarity. Ext.: $^a$self-extraction; $^b$cross-model (Mistral-7B extracts FOL from target traces). $^*$Interim ($n = 95/204$). All models exhibit $\\text{BR} > 1$; BR estimates for high-accuracy models ($n_\\text{err} < 40$) have wider confidence intervals; Gemini-2.5-Pro ($n_\\text{err} \\approx 12$) should be interpreted with particular caution.}\n\\label{tab:frontier-scb}\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Ext. & SC (\\%) & $n_\\text{err}$ & BR & $\\kappa_{\\text{proc}}$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & $^a$ & 55.88 & 90 & 4.97 & 0.690 \\\\\nLLaMA-8B & $^a$ & \\textrm{---} & \\textrm{---} & 2.88 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\nQwen-14B & $^a$ & 75.49 & 50 & 3.93 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nR1-Distill-8B & $^b$ & 74.51 & 52 & 2.92 & 0.248 \\\\\ngpt-4o & $^a$ & 73.91 & 53 & 2.94 & 0.790 \\\\\ngpt-4.1 & $^b$ & 82.84 & 35 & 2.70 & 0.792 \\\\\ngpt-5.5 & $^b$ & 84.31 & 32 & 3.48 & 0.563 \\\\\no3 & $^b$ & 82.65 & 35 & 1.66 & 0.596 \\\\\nGemini-2.5-Pro$^*$ & $^b$ & 87.37 & 12 & 2.79 & 0.790 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Two controls rule out answer leakage. BR is uniform across early, mid, and late reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$); high-consensus errors amplify to $\\text{BR} = 18.8\\times$ versus $2.7\\times$ for low-consensus ($p < 0.001$). Removing the answer sentence from traces before extraction yields no BR decrease (Wilcoxon $p \\geq 0.12$) despite significant accuracy loss (McNemar $p < 0.013$). SCB reflects a distributional prior embedded throughout reasoning, not answer contamination.\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We quantify reasoning-process overlap via ProcessSim, the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace vectors (Eq.~\\ref{eq:process-sim}, Appendix~\\ref{app:process-gradient}). ProcessSim captures lexical overlap; deeper structure measures may reveal additional patterns, so we treat it as supporting rather than primary evidence. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B). Under cross-model extraction, SICA gain decreases monotonically with ProcessSim ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.008$; Table~\\ref{tab:process-gradient}), modulated by self-bias severity.\n\n\nCCR analysis confirms the same pattern: cross-architecture NLI yields $\\text{CCR} > 0$ while same-model SICA yields $\\text{CCR} < 0$, with CCR correlating with combo gain ($\\rho = 0.84$, $p = 0.014$; Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}).\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free remediation strategies spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). Four representative failure modes illuminate the independence-accuracy trade-off. \\emph{Debate} ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $\\Delta = -18.6$\\,pp) successfully breaks trace correlation but collapses accuracy---same-model diversity and quality are at odds. \\emph{Cross-model extraction} (using a different LLM as extractor) decreases $\\kappa$ slightly but $\\Delta \\approx 0$, because same-family models share systematic biases (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). \\emph{Contrastive prompting} produces imbalanced output (94 pro vs.\\ 65 con constraints), indicating confirmation bias transfers to constraint generation. The \\emph{oracle ceiling} ($+29.90$\\,pp with gold constraints) proves the mechanism is sound; self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\%, localizing the bottleneck to extraction quality. All non-debate strategies cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling across three families. No individual strategy achieves significance after correction (Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}). Trained extractors remain the most promising direction.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_interventions_compact.pdf}\n\\caption{Twenty-three of 27 training-free strategies across four categories (four additional strategies tested but omitted for clarity). All produce neutral-to-negative $\\Delta$ vs.\\ SC. Grey band: $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis: verification.}\n\\looseness=-1 The observations and prediction of \\S\\ref{sec:mirage} are confirmed. Observations O1--O2, initially discovered on Mistral-7B, generalize: BR $> 1$ across three self-extraction models, six cross-model pairs, and all eight cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); 27 training-free strategies fail to reduce BR below 1.0 (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). Prediction P1 is validated: cross-architecture NLI escapes the mirage when the verifier exceeds the generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n",

        "totalLines": 70
      }

        "content": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:exp-setup}\n\nWe evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models, three reasoning domains, and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), with diagnostic analyses of confirmation bias and process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}).\n\n\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). We additionally evaluate SCB universality via cross-model extraction on five frontier models (gpt-4o, gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}), where Mistral-7B extracts constraints from the target model's traces since closed-source APIs do not expose internals suitable for self-extraction.\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} (exploratory, $n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\\%$).\n\n\\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s. McNemar exact test ($\\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples) for $n \\geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\\% of overhead.\n\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\\label{sec:main-results}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all conditions sorted by SC baseline, revealing three observation-driven accuracy regimes.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{SICA vs.\\ SC across all conditions (sorted by SC). No non-degenerate condition shows a significant gain after BH correction ($m{=}16$). $\\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). Symbols: $^b$gold ICL; $\\dagger$\\,$n < 100$; \\textbf{bold}: $p < 0.05$; {*}\\,uncorrected $p < 0.05$ (BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$); {***}\\,oracle $p < 0.001$; $^{3s}$\\,3-seed; $^{4s}$\\,4-seed confirmatory (unmarked: single-seed exploratory).}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Domain & $n$ & SC (\\%) & SICA (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\\leq$ 40\\%)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\\sim$50--60\\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 51.00 & 51.50 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) & 204 & 54.41 & 57.35 & $+$2.94 & 0.070 \\\\\nMistral-7B$^{3s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 56.53 & 56.70 & $+$0.16 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s456) & 204 & 55.88 & 55.88 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s789) & 204 & 59.31 & 56.86 & $-$2.45 & 0.267 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\\geq$ 60\\%)}} \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 66.18 & 61.76 & $-$4.41 & 0.093 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO (ICL)$^b$ & 199 & 74.37 & 74.87 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 74.00 & 71.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & 74.51 & $-$0.98 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 78.50 & 78.00 & $-$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 84.80 & 84.31 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.00 & 85.67 & $+$0.67 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B (think) & FOLIO & 204 & 86.27 & 86.76 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Degenerate ($C_0 < 5\\%$)}} \\\\\nQwQ-32B & AIME 2024$^\\dagger$ & 22 & 72.70 & 72.70 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & GSM8K$^\\dagger$ & 30 & 96.67 & 96.67 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Oracle upper bound (gold logical annotations + Z3)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & \\textbf{85.78} & $\\mathbf{+29.90}$ & $1.0{\\times}10^{-13}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & \\textbf{89.71} & $\\mathbf{+14.22}$ & $2.0{\\times}10^{-7}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & ProofWriter & 600 & 74.00 & \\textbf{91.17} & $\\mathbf{+17.17}$ & $9.21{\\times}10^{-15}${***} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\looseness=-1 No non-degenerate condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--60\\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. An aggregate sign test across self-extraction conditions yields 5 positive and 10 negative $\\Delta$ values ($p = 0.30$, two-sided); including all conditions gives 8+/15$-$ ($p = 0.21$). Three-seed replication on Mistral-7B confirms seed-level noise: two of three seeds produce null or negative $\\Delta$ (mean $+0.16 \\pm 2.70$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}). Four-seed replication on Qwen2.5-14B $\\times$ FOLIO confirms the single-seed pattern: $\\Delta \\in [0.00, -2.01]$\\,pp (mean $-1.24 \\pm 0.86$; Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}). TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp confirm 4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative), 10 underpowered. For the 10 underpowered conditions, our tests cannot rule out effects smaller than 1.86--4.84\\,pp at 80\\% power; the largest study ($n{=}804$) achieves MDE $= 1.86$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence for small effects. Margin sensitivity is robust: at $\\pm 1$\\,pp, 0/16 equivalent; at $\\pm 3$\\,pp, 10/16 equivalent; no margin yields a significant positive effect (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:tost-sensitivity}). We adopt $\\pm 2$\\,pp based on the lower bound of SC literature gains (2--5\\,pp \\citep{wang2023sc}), acknowledging this choice is anchored to SC effect sizes. We trace the failure below.\n\n\n\\subsection{Oracle Control}\n\\label{sec:oracle}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Replacing self-extracted constraints with gold annotations (FOLIO logical formulae, ProofWriter DSL), Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+14.22$\\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \\times 10^{-7}$), and $+17.17$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter). The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted $\\Delta$ ($+3.43$\\,pp) captures only 11.5\\% of its oracle ceiling. For Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter, the gap is wider still: self-extraction degrades accuracy ($-2.50$\\,pp) against an oracle ceiling of $+17.17$\\,pp, indicating that stronger models do not self-extract more effectively.\n\n\n",

        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 75
      }
    },

{
  "parentUuid": "82169708-e2b7-4e08-88da-ce049510f8ca",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/related_work.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/related_work.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}\nSC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \\citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \\citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \\citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early; \\citet{byerly2026selfconsistency} further analyze SC's limitations in mathematical reasoning. Alternative aggregation strategies beyond majority voting \\citep{ai2025beyondmajority} have been proposed but do not address the underlying correlation problem. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\n\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nWe distinguish three paradigms. \\emph{Specification-generation} (Logic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm}, LINC \\citep{olausson2023linc}, SatLM \\citep{ye2024satlm}) translates problem descriptions into formal specifications before any answer is generated; LINC documents ${\\sim}$40\\% translation errors even at this level. \\emph{Program-generation} \\citep{gao2023pal,chen2023program,lyu2023faithful} produces executable code from problem statements. Both paradigms access the problem before generating candidate answers. \\emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc on reasoning traces that already contain candidates, inheriting confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). SatLM provides a natural control: it succeeds because problem-level formalization precedes answer generation, whereas trace-level extraction cannot avoid contamination (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). Recent extensions \\citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku} introduce capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization degrades with complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\\,pp FOLIO, $+17.17$\\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}\nScaling test-time computation through additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling} or structured search \\citep{yao2023tree} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \\citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to correlated same-model voting: QwQ-32B produces $\\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-verification \\citep{weng2023selfverification}, chain-of-verification \\citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, tool-augmented critique \\citep{gou2024critic}, and formal CoT verification \\citep{zhu2025vericot} report gains, but none systematically tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers. Recent self-verification methods in test-time scaling \\citep{TBD_SETS} show positive results using trained verification signals, contrasting with our negative findings for untrained symbolic extraction; multi-agent verification approaches \\citep{TBD_MAV} align with our cross-architecture evidence that structurally independent verifiers can escape SCB (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). We find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; \\citet{valmeekam2023llms} further show fundamental planning limitations that compound self-verification failures. Our diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\\kappa$) predict when trace-aggregation will fail.\n\n\\paragraph{CJT and positioning.}\nCJT \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size. Classical ensemble diversity theory \\citep{kuncheva2003measures, brown2005diversity} formalizes this intuition: diversity measures (e.g., $\\kappa$-statistic, disagreement) predict ensemble gain. Our $K_\\text{eff}$ adapts Fleiss' $\\kappa$ for this purpose. \\citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. \\citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs. Concurrent work by \\citet{kuai2026independent} proposes BEI and CIG to audit cross-model behavioral dependencies; our process-$\\kappa$ and $K_\\text{eff}$ are complementary, operating at the reasoning-process level within same-model traces and identifying a specific causal mechanism (SCB) that behavioral metrics miss.\nPrior self-correction studies \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute quantitative diagnostics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_D$). SCB is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \\citep{turpin2024language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}: the latter concern whether stated reasoning reflects actual computation, while SCB concerns whether \\emph{self-extracted formal constraints} mirror the model's answer distribution. Formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$)---invisible to prior work on natural-language self-correction.",
        "numLines": 15,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 15
      }
    },

{

  "attachment": {

    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/method.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Background \\& Setup}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}\n\\label{sec:condorcet}\n\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees majority convergence under independent voters \\citep{austensmith1996}, but LLM traces violate this independence. Following \\citet{ladha1992correlated} and \\citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:effk}\nK_{\\text{eff}} = K \\,/\\, [1 + (K{-}1) \\cdot \\kappa].\n\\end{equation} In our experiments (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ yields $K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2$--$3.6$ for $K{=}12$, serving as an ordinal diagnostic of vote diversity rather than a literal effective sample size. This independence limitation motivates our investigation.\n\n\\subsection{SICA Pipeline}\n\\label{sec:pipeline}\n\nSymbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\n\n\\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\\{t_1, \\ldots, t_K\\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.\n\n\\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts logical constraints from each trace at $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompts}); in practice, 99.0\\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\\% using true quantifiers. The \\emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence (global average deduplication ratio: 46.0\\%); each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:maxsat}\n\\mathcal{C}^* = \\arg\\max_{\\mathcal{S} \\subseteq \\mathcal{C}} \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{S}} w_c \\quad \\text{s.t.} \\quad \\mathcal{S} \\text{ is satisfiable.}\n\\end{equation}\nZ3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\\texttt{answer\\_a} == \\texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.\n\n\\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus} = \\mathcal{C} \\setminus \\mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:score}\n\\text{score}(a) = \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^*} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a) - \\alpha \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \\arg\\max_{a} \\text{score}(a)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:degenerate}\n\\text{score}(a) \\approx \\sum_{\\substack{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^* \\\\ \\text{source}(c) \\to a}} w_c,\n\\end{equation}\nequivalent to majority voting. We show in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.\n\n\\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\\{q : |\\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\\}| / |\\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC. $\\alpha = 0.5$ is not critical (${\\pm}0.49$\\,pp across $\\alpha \\in [0, 1]$).\n\n\\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}\n\\label{sec:mirage}\n\nWe hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. This \\textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}). We quantify mirage intensity via the \\emph{Bias Ratio}:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:mirage}\n\\text{BR}(M,T) = \\frac{P(c \\text{ supports } a_M \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)}{P(c \\text{ supports } a^* \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)},\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. $\\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints project model bias rather than provide independent verification. The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). If traces are unfaithful \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. Two observations motivate the hypothesis: (O1)~BR $> 1.0$ across all tested self-extraction conditions (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (O2)~no training-free remediation reduces BR below $1.0$ (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation})---both initially observed on Mistral-7B and subsequently confirmed across nine models. These lead to a testable prediction: (P1)~structurally independent verification should break SCB (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced, colback=gray!5, colframe=gray!50, boxrule=0.3pt, left=3pt, right=3pt, top=1pt, bottom=1pt, before skip=4pt, after skip=4pt]\n\\textbf{Empirical Finding 1} (Self-Extraction Ceiling).\\label{prop:ceiling}\nFor generator $M$ with $\\text{BR}(M) > 1$ and oracle improvement $\\Delta_\\text{oracle}$, self-extracted constraints improve SC by at most $\\Delta_\\text{max}^\\text{self}$, empirically bounded by $\\Delta_\\text{oracle} / \\text{BR}(M)$.\n\\end{tcolorbox}\n\n\\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced, colback=gray!5, colframe=gray!50, boxrule=0.3pt, left=3pt, right=3pt, top=1pt, bottom=1pt, before skip=4pt, after skip=4pt]\n\\textbf{Empirical Finding 2} (Capability Gap Condition).\\label{cor:capability_gap}\nLet $G$ be a generator with SC accuracy $\\text{SC}_G$ and let $V$ be an external verifier with standalone accuracy $V_\\text{acc}$ and inter-system correlation $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$. Combo gain $\\Delta_\\text{combo} - \\Delta_\\text{SC}$ is monotonically increasing in the capability gap $(V_\\text{acc} - \\text{SC}_G)$.\n\\end{tcolorbox}\n\n\\textit{Empirical support.} From Finding~1, adding $V$ with $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$ increases $K_\\text{eff}^\\text{combo}$, but incorrect verifier votes counteract gains when $V_\\text{acc} < \\text{SC}_G$. Empirically, combo gain is monotonically ordered by the capability gap ($p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nWe also define a Conditional Correction Ratio (CCR, Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}) that summarizes these diagnostics.\n",
        "numLines": 68,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 68
      }
